# PLAN — the single source of truth (projects · tasks · ETAs)

**This file is THE plan.** Every task lives here, under a project, with an `ETA` date and a `status`.
The long-horizon plan (the 6/22 launch) lives here too — as dated tasks, not a separate doc. There is no
second plan surface: the calendar is phone reminders only, the harness #-task list is a session scratch
mirror, `CHANGELOG.md`/git hold history. If it's planned, it's a line here.

**Line format** (strict — parsed by `scripts/check_plan.py`):
`- #<id> | <YYYY-MM-DD ETA> | <status> | <title>`   ·  status ∈ pending · in_progress · blocked

**The two rituals operate ONLY on this file:**
- **OPEN** (every session): run `python scripts/check_plan.py --today` → it prints OVERDUE + due-today open
  tasks. That list IS the day's plan. State it before reacting to anything.
- **CLOSE** (every session): every open task's ETA must be ≥ today. Rebump any past-due one to a real
  future date (or close it). File every new item as a line here under a project. Then commit — the
  pre-commit gate (`check_plan.py`) REFUSES the commit if any open task lacks a project, an ETA, or has a
  past ETA, or if an open harness task is missing from this file. No gaps, mechanically.

_Last CLOSE: 2026-06-16._

---

## Live-money cutover — MAGNA53 (the GO-direction gates)

- #503 | 2026-08-04 | in_progress | 🔴 **LIVE COHORT IS 0-FOR-9 (−7.50R) — per-trade forensic: variance, or an upstream defect?** Broken out of #454 at the operator's call 2026-07-24: nine losses on real money is not a footnote to a calibration task. FACTS (verified vs prod 7/24, `mi_live_trades` account_mode='live' status='closed'): **9 closed, 0 wins, losing streak 9, cum −7.50R (−$180), mean −0.833R**, spanning 2026-07-06 → 07-24; **9/9 in NON-BULL regimes** the #268b envelope never priced (`docs/analysis/454_band_review_2026-07-24.md`). **THE QUESTION IS NOT 'RETUNE'** — at n=9 nothing clears CHANGE_PROCESS rule 1, and the band review already ruled KEEP. The question is diagnostic: **is this normal variance for a 20-30%-win-rate breakout system, or is something upstream broken?** Prior arithmetic to test, not assume: P(9 straight losses) ≈ 4% @30% WR · 7.5% @25% · 13% @20% — uncommon but NOT self-evidently diagnostic. Note the mean loss (−0.833R) is TIGHTER than −1R, i.e. **stops are working; the anomaly is the total absence of winners**, where ~2-3 were expected. DoD = a per-trade forensic on all 9 (entry price vs plan · MFE reached before exit · exit reason · holding period · regime label at alert), each classified into exactly one of: **(a) variance** · **(b) entry quality** — wrong names selected · **(c) entry mechanics** — bad fill / chase / no-trigger (composes #414 D2 gap-through + #500's price-aware fix, live since 7/23) · **(d) exit discipline** — stopped or time-stopped before the move (composes #306 giveback/peak-lock, +$8,075 lock-attributable offline) · **(e) regime** — a momentum system correctly finding nothing in a hostile tape. Output = either "consistent with variance, continue" WITH the MFE evidence that supports it, or a NAMED upstream defect with its fix routed to the owning task. ⚠ **TIME-CRITICAL LINK**: the kill/scale bands mechanically ACTIVATE at n=20 (projected ≈ 2026-08-20, verified cadence 9 closes / 15 trading days) and the band review's arithmetic says **REDUCE at activation is the modal outcome** (needs only ≤ −0.59R over the next 11; current mean −0.83R) — landing a Bull-calibrated REDUCE on a non-Bull tape. Knowing WHICH of (a)-(e) is operating before that fires is the point of doing this now, not at n=20. Route: **Fable** (complex analysis) per the operating model; read-only, no live change; any resulting fix is its own CHANGE_PROCESS + sign-off (THE LINE). Refs: `mi_live_trades`, `mi_ep_alerts`, `mi_market_regime`, `mi_orb_shadow_trades`, #454 (parent), #414, #306, #500, #452. >> ✅ **FORENSIC DELIVERED 2026-07-25** (`docs/analysis/503_zero_for_nine_forensic_2026-07-25.md`, Fable; I verified the load-bearing numbers vs prod myself). **VERDICT: not pure variance, not a broken detector — a 3-part decomposition.** (1) **SELECTION IS ALIVE**: MFE (from `highest_price_seen`, code-verified as a real tracker) shows winners occurred at roughly the calibrated rate — **MANE +7.92R**, NVCR +2.00R, WKC +0.90R, THC +0.64R, WULF +0.48R (plus open SMCI +3.21R). Entry mechanics clean on all 9 (max chase +0.52%, the designed limit); class (c) = ZERO. (2) **THE STRUCTURAL GAP IS EXIT-SIDE → #306**: NO profit protection exists on Days 1-2 (SMA trail needs ≥10 closes · partials start Day 3 · the 9:35 'refresh' re-places the SAME stop · the built giveback hook is DEFAULT-OFF with no live caller). Both runners fully reclaimed: **MANE 7.92R → −0.23R**, NVCR 2.00R → −1.00R. Honest counterfactual: the SIGNED close-below giveback saves only MANE (~+1.7R; cohort −7.5R → −5.8R); reaching NVCR/SMCI needs an INTRADAY-arm variant — that arm-basis fork is #306's decision and now has first live-money evidence. (3) residual −5.8R reads as regime-priced variance. Tally: (a)×1 (b)×3 (c)×0 (d)×3 (e)×2; all 9 exits `stop_hit`, none reached Day 3. **⚠ CORRECTION TO THIS TASK'S OWN PRIOR (which I wrote): 'mean −0.833R is TIGHTER than −1R ⇒ stops working better than designed' is a DENOMINATOR ARTIFACT.** `risk_dollars` is BUDGETED risk; on DEPLOYED risk (`shares×(entry−hard_stop)`, after integer-floor + the 20% notional cap) I measured mean **−0.930R**, median **−1.006R**, **8 of 9 at full stop**. Stops hit exactly as designed — the 'tighter' framing was wrong and the inference built on it is discarded. Participation note (descriptive, n=4, no change proposed): 4 of 9 entries BYPASSED the regime-raised threshold (judge-promote ×2, earnings-override ×2) and all 4 lost (−3.78R, max MFE +0.90R). The partial is gated at **hold day 3**, and: live magna53 avg hold **1.5 days**, only **1 of 12** ever reached day 3, **0 partials fired ever**, kept −0.92R · paper 9m_day2 avg hold **8.0 days**, 5 of 7 reached day 3, 4 partials, kept **+0.20R** · paper magna53 avg hold 3.2 days, 9 of 24, 7 partials, kept −0.80R. **So 9M is NOT better at exiting** (operator's instinct that it might be a fluke — it isn't, it's mechanical): it HOLDS 8 days instead of 1.5, so it survives to reach the rule that already exists. ⚠ **CONFOUND, stated not glossed: the partial group REACHED 5.23R vs 0.90R — those were already better trades, and surviving to day 3 SELECTS for winners. Partials firing may be a CONSEQUENCE of a trade working, not a cause. What is NOT confounded: a mechanism requiring day 3 cannot help a cohort that dies at day 1.5.** ⚠ And even when it fires it is LEAKY — the partial group keeps only ~19% of what it reached. ▶ **WORKSTREAM 2 (Friday) now has a sharper question than 'what exit rule?': should the partial be gated on DAYS at all, or on R-REACHED? QBTS hit +3.74R on day 1 and the rule could not see it.** ETA 2026-07-31 = workstream 2. >> ✅ **W1 + W2 BOTH DONE 2026-07-30, all deployed + verified-live.** Recorder live (43 records, scheduled 17:46 job ran CLEAN); replay harness `scripts/probes/_508_exit_rule_replay.py`. ⚖ **W2 VERDICT: taking something at ≥+2R reached beats BOTH the deployed day-3 rule and doing nothing — consistently across all 3 cohorts, both axes, and the artifact-excluded subset (n=37). But the cohort has ZERO live winners, so every candidate's COST side is priced from 2 PAPER winners (one dominating), and the R-variants (1R/1.5R/2R/3R; ⅓/½/all) sit INSIDE SINGLE-TRADE NOISE. It cannot pick a level, and a fitted winner from that table would be the exact overfit CHANGE_PROCESS exists to prevent.** ⚠ **BIGGER FINDING — R IS NOT A FIXED UNIT, so the operator's '+3R partial' cannot work as stated until R is calibrated: live stop widths span **0.14×–0.97× of the ticker's own 20d ADR** (7× spread). MANE 7.92R and QBTS 3.74R were the SAME move (8.76% vs 8.64%, both ~1.2 ADR); NVCR made the LARGEST real move of the cohort (2.35 ADR) and scored the LOWEST R of the four movers. So R mostly measures STOP TIGHTNESS — a +3R trigger would fire constantly on tight-stopped names and never on wide-stopped ones, and the operator's tuning axes (regime · biotech/small-cap character) would be confounded by stop width before they could be read.** FIXED THE MEASUREMENT, not the rule: `stop_pct` / `stop_per_adr` / `peak_adr` / `realized_adr` now recorded per trade from strictly PRE-alert bars (NULL when history is short — absent, never fabricated). Done NOW because 25 more trades on an uncalibrated R would leave us where we are with more rows. Operator argued for shipping now rather than parking, and **his argument is better than my parking rec**: the incumbent fires 1 in 12 live trades (+0.09R) vs +0.47R for the proposal, and — the point I had underweighted — **shipping costs us NOTHING in evidence, because the recorder logs peak/giveback regardless of which rule is live and the replay scores all 34 candidates counterfactually.** ⚠ **TWO PROCESS ITEMS SURFACED, both his call: (1) there is NO SSoT for exit discipline** — `docs/setups/` covers detection + safeguards only, so CHANGE_PROCESS r6/r7 have nothing to update; the proposal includes creating `docs/setups/exit_discipline.md`. **(2) CHANGE_PROCESS r5 requires shadow/paper field-validation BEFORE live, which conflicts with shipping now** — options (a) follow r5 and wait weeks at ~1.5 trades/wk, (b) ship live treating the 36-trade replay as the validation, (c) ship live with PRE-COMMITTED reversion triggers so the live phase IS the validation. I did not choose; the proposal is written for (c) because it alone both ships and keeps r5's intent. ⚠ Honest limits in the doc, not buried: live cohort has ZERO winners so every gain is loss-reduction; **on PAPER the incumbent BEATS the proposal (+0.36 vs +0.27)** and paper is the only cohort with winners; and the shadow control (0 winners across BULL months, no broker) says the real problem is upstream — **this makes losses smaller, it will not make the strategy profitable, and must not be signed on that basis.** Verified vs code: deployed-rule quote exact; `scale_fraction` controls SIZE not trigger so it can't carry the change; `exit_logic.py` IS execution-loaded so `deploy.sh execution` is required. >> ✅ **NO-MONEY PIECES BUILT 2026-08-01** (the parts that need no sign-off): **(1) `docs/setups/exit_discipline.md` CREATED** — the SSoT that did not exist, documenting the deployed rule INCLUDING its undocumented day-5 unconditional branch (a de-facto full time-exit), the DAILY decision cadence, and 4 known limitations. **(2) Profit-take DECISION now Telegrams** — `run_partial_exits` had ZERO Telegram calls, so a partial the system CHOSE reached him only as a downstream broker fill with no why/level; now announces ticker · day · close · R · shares · new stop, self-catching so a notify failure can never abort the sell. **(3) `first_live_winner` review added** — nothing watched for the single event that most changes this analysis; predicate verified against prod (returns 0). ⚠ **MY OWN BUG, caught by AST not by tests: the first notification referenced `entry`/`risk`/`remaining`, NONE of which are bound in that function** — inside a try/except it would have raised NameError and silently NEVER notified while looking shipped. The 2 proposals both look real (Bitcoin miners→AI data centers 7/20; AI memory demand 6/25). **18 live opportunities in 7 weeks is too thin to judge the lane on — it has not been given enough chances to fail.** ▶ **OPERATOR RULED (2026-07-27): KEEP + keep collecting — and investigate whether the GROUPING PATH itself is right ('are we discovering the subtle theme for 2+ EPs well').** Audit commissioned → `docs/analysis/167_lane2_grouping_quality_2026-07-27.md`: the core test is OBJECTIVE, not LLM-grading-LLM — for each no-story day, did those tickers LATER land in the same theme (any lane) or co-move market-adjusted? Plus catalyst-text richness (leading hypothesis: the input is too thin for a subtle link to be visible), prompt bias ('truly' + an explicit empty-set escape), and whether a lone alert could join an ALREADY-ACTIVE cohort instead of being dropped. **NEW ETA 2026-09-08 = a RUN-COUNT horizon, not a date roll** — at ~4-5 live runs/wk this reaches ~50 forward runs, enough to judge the grouping. **Stop treating this as active weekly work; it accrues.** [ok:operator-ruled keep-and-accrue — run-count horizon (~50 forward runs), not a date roll; the lane is a rare-event detector and 18 live runs cannot judge it, 2026-07-27→2026-09-08] >> 🔔 **TRIGGER IS NOW DATA, NOT A DATE (operator asked 2026-07-27: "what's the trigger to review this going forward?" — fair, my 09-08 ETA was still a calendar).** Registered `data_gated_reviews::lane2_narrative_grouping_quality` — predicate counts FORWARD `narrative_theme_discovery_ran` events since 2026-06-07, **threshold 50** (33 today, ~4-5/wk). Counts RUNS not proposals, deliberately: a rare-event detector cannot be judged on output volume, only on the number of live OPPORTUNITIES it has had. `earliest_review_date` set to registration day so it is NON-BLOCKING — firing is AND, and a future date could only delay a review whose evidence is already in (the calendar-block pattern ruled against 7/26). The 09-08 ETA is now just a backstop for the PLAN gate; the REVIEW fires on run count. >> 🔬 **GROUPING AUDIT DONE 2026-07-27 — `docs/analysis/167_lane2_grouping_quality_2026-07-27.md`. VERDICT: PRECISE BUT SHALLOW — it can only group when the shared story is explicitly NAMED inside both 280-char snippets, so it cannot DISCOVER a subtle theme; it never sees the evidence.** ⚖ **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED (I verified independently): the lane reads ~4% of the evidence already on the row.** Measured on the 62 forward-era qualifying alerts: `catalyst` avg length 500 = max 500 → **every single one is hard-truncated at 500**, and the prompt keeps only **280**; meanwhile `grounded_text` is populated on 50/62 (81%) at a **median 7,413 chars** and is UNUSED. 11/54 texts (20%) are 'no catalyst' boilerplate or misidentified tickers (KC graded as wheat futures — it is Kingsoft Cloud). ✅ **PROMPT BIAS TESTED AND DISCONFIRMED as the primary driver** — a blind, neutrally-framed Sonnet (editorial framing, no 'truly', no empty-list escape, identical inputs) REPRODUCED EVERY production decline. Rewording buys no recall; the flaw is the INPUT, not the wording. **CORE TEST (objective, not LLM-grading-LLM):** of 16 no-story days, 13 old enough to judge → **3 genuine misses · 1 borderline · 9 correctly declined**, via later shared-theme membership (0 pairs, all lanes) + SPY-adjusted pairwise correlation vs a 1,509-pair null (p95 = +0.58). The misses: **06-17 AEHR+JBL** (corr +0.71 > p95; both texts name AI-infrastructure IN the visible 280 — same breadth as the ACCEPTED MU+SNX grouping) · **07-14 CLSK+TSEM** (CLSK = a bitcoin miner signing a $6.6B AI-data-center lease = **the EXACT narrative accepted 6 days later for HUT+IREN**; CLSK was never captured by ANY lane) · 06-15 three precious-metals miners where 2 of 3 texts said 'no identifiable catalyst' (input-caused). **All misses share one shape: demand-side cross-sector cycles written up as company events.** PRECISION IS INTACT — both accepted proposals verified real, KYMR correctly excluded. **The <2 gate killed WULF 07-06 (ep 96, 20-yr Anthropic lease) and DOCN 07-07 (ep 115)**; the miner-pivot cohort ACCRETED ACROSS DAYS (WULF→CLSK→HUT/IREN, July pairwise corr +0.47..+0.87) — structurally invisible through a same-day pinhole, and the lane caught the last 2 members 2 weeks late. ⚖ **5 PROPOSALS, operator-gated (detection ⇒ THE LINE + CHANGE_PROCESS), evidence-ordered:** (1) feed `grounded_text`/`claude_analysis` instead of `catalyst[:280]`; (2) rolling 5-10 day candidate window (also fixes most lone-alert drops); (3) pass the active theme/narrative roster for joiner proposals (anti-circularity wall preserved); (4) demand-side prompt instruction ONLY — do NOT soften 'truly', precision is 2/2 and must be protected; (5) separate ticket for the ~6% ticker-misID upstream. Guard: replay + hold precision via the existing `evaluate_narrative_themes` harness. ⚠ **SAMPLE HONESTY: N=13 cannot separate a 23% from a 10% miss rate (~4-5 more months would) — the proposals stand on the TRACED MECHANISM (input + window), not on the rate.** >> 🔨 **v2 BUILT DARK 2026-07-27 — flag `lane2_grouping_v2` (mi_safeguard_state), FAIL-CLOSED OFF, NOT deployed, NOT flipped.** OFF is byte-identical to v1, pinned by a test comparing the built prompt against an independent FROZEN copy of the pre-change template. Both ruled changes built: rich input (`grounded_text`→`claude_analysis`→`catalyst`, never crashes, per-ticker source tag, and every v2 audit row carries `input grounded=G analysis=A catalyst=C` so a DEGRADED day is distinguishable) + **10 TRADING-day window** via `prev_trading_days` — verified `_lane2_window_start(2026-07-20) == 2026-07-06`, exactly the operator's measured WULF→HUT/IREN chain (test also pins that CALENDAR math would land on 07-10 and be WRONG). Dedup: per ticker keep highest ep_score, tie→latest (same semantics as `get_today_ep_alerts` extended across days; the strongest alert carries the substantive evidence). Anchor set computed BEFORE dedup; every proposal must contain ≥1 TODAY name — enforced in CODE, not just prompted. Prompt text extracted to a shared constant used VERBATIM by both paths (bias was disconfirmed — never reworded). ⚖ **A BUILT ASSUMPTION WAS FALSIFIED BY THE DATA AND CORRECTED:** the agent first used a 2,500-char head budget; the replay showed SEC/XBRL boilerplate fills the doc HEAD while the linking evidence sits MID-doc (CLSK 'AI'@6394 · JBL 'AI'@4430 · SNX 'AI'@6593 · WULF 'data center'@2653) and the story-naming web synthesis is LAST in `build_grounded_text`'s order — **a head slice would have re-created the exact misses.** Now 10,000 chars = the full document in practice (era max 9,615); a test pins deep-in-doc retention. **DETERMINISTIC REPLAY RAN** (2026-06-08→07-24, 35 weekdays, real prod data): **27 days pass the v2 gate vs 18 v1 grouped runs** — all 9 lone-alert drops now group (incl. WULF 07-06 ep96, DOCN 07-07 ep115); 07-14 now co-contains CLSK+TSEM+WULF with the lease text; 07-20 shows the full 4-name chain for the first time; 06-17 has JBL's Adani 8-K body. ⚠ **06-15 precious-metals HONESTLY NOT EXPECTED to be caught** — 2 of 3 rows literally say 'no identifiable catalyst'; that miss class is UPSTREAM input integrity (audit proposal 5, out of scope). ⚠ **THE LLM LEG WAS NOT RUN — no ANTHROPIC_API_KEY in the build environment; the agent said so rather than implying a replay it didn't do.** Driver staged: `scripts/probes/_replay_lane2_v2.py`, one command, measured **~504k input tokens ≈ $1.67** for all 27 days. Its per-day output IS the 'every new cohort' list the operator must review. Prod run cost when ON: ~$0.08-0.11/night. Tests 3835 (+10). ⚠⚠ **NEWLY SURFACED, VERIFIED BY ME: `narrative_cogap` IS in `db.AUTO_PROMOTE_THEME_SOURCES` (db.py:5838) — so a flipped-ON v2 cohort with enough members (e.g. the 4-name miner chain) could AUTO-PROMOTE into LIVE `mi_themes` via the nightly gate. The flip is therefore not just judge-affecting; it can CREATE LIVE THEMES.** Must be named in the sign-off digest. **FLIP REQUIRES: (1) the $1.67 LLM replay + operator review of every new cohort (CHANGE_PROCESS r3 — classifying wins vs false positives is the operator's call, precision is 2-for-2 and outranks recall); (2) a FRESH judge-robustness eval — the ADR-0030 gate WILL fire on the grade-surface drift, BY DESIGN, never suppress it; (3) sign-off + change-log status flip.**

[blocked:re-eval DATA-gated (≥10 themed-MODERATE settled, NOT calendar) — see the keep-rationale doc — 7/19] >> CLOSE 7/21: stays [blocked:] data-gated (≥10 themed-MODERATE settled + stable theme-engine window); re-dated to the theme_axis_boost_reeval window. >> ⚠ **GATE CORRECTED 7/25 (I was wrong on 7/25 AM — I called this 'the most promising candidate' off the 476-row total).** The gate is ≥10 **themed-MODERATE settled**; the actual population is HIGH themed 59 · HIGH themeless 414 · **MODERATE themeless 3 · MODERATE THEMED 0**. So it is **0/10**, not nearly-there. Note the shape: only 3 of 476 rows are MODERATE at all and every one is themeless — like #310, this gate may be structurally slow rather than merely early. Earliest review date is 2026-09-15 regardless. >> 🔑 **GATE RE-POINTED + PREMISE TESTED 2026-07-26 (operator ruled option b).** ROOT CAUSE of the 0/10 stall, measured: (1) MODERATE was only instrumented from **2026-07-15** (11 days of data); (2) **87% of EP alerts genuinely have NO theme — and that is CORRECT, not under-detection**: of 417 `themeless_flag=true` rows, only **1** had its ticker in a live theme within 3 days. So themed-MODERATE = rare × rare ≈ **0.65/month → ~15 MONTHS to reach 10.** Nothing to fix; the GATE was set assuming a rate that does not exist. >> **So we tested the boost's PREMISE on the population that DOES exist (themed HIGH n=59 vs themeless HIGH n=414, fwd-5d from alert-date close): themed mean +0.50% / median −1.70% / 44% win vs themeless mean −0.60% / median −0.43% / 46% win.** Better mean, WORSE median, slightly lower win-rate → **FAILS the both-measures bar** (the same profile as the #170 re-setup finding: thin right tail, worse typical case). ⚠ **Evidence AGAINST, not proof:** this tests CORRELATION (does theme predict edge?) on an already-selected HIGH cohort, whereas the boost is designed for the MARGIN (lift themed MODERATE → HIGH). Theme signal can be redundant among HIGHs and still matter at the margin — but there is no themed-MODERATE population to check that on, and won't be for ~15 months. Also close-to-close fwd, which understates intraday. **CONSEQUENCE: do NOT flip #335 on current evidence**; the premise does not clear its own bar. And #301/#329 no longer wait on a 15-month gate — they decouple with a 'no' rather than parking indefinitely. >> ⛔ **CONCLUSION RETRACTED SAME DAY — my 'do NOT flip' read does not stand (operator, 3 grounds, all correct).** (a) **My themeless check was CIRCULAR.** I verified `themeless_flag` matches `mi_themes` and concluded 87% themeless is 'correct, not under-detection'. That only proves our DETECTOR AGREES WITH ITSELF. **Not detecting a theme ≠ no theme exists** — which is exactly why the EP signal is meant to FEED BACK INTO theme discovery, not merely consume it. The 15-month projection inherits that error. (b) **REGIME CONFOUND**: the themed-HIGH test ran over a window that is mostly Correcting/Choppy, when hot/themed names are hit HARDEST — measuring theme value in a correction is biased against themes by construction. (c) **I evaluated the wrong thing.** I tested themes on ONE narrow use (the MODERATE→HIGH grade boost) and called the axis marginal. The real value is PORTFOLIO-LEVEL: **boost capital allocation toward hot areas · arbitrate competing EPs for limited slots · expand slots for a strong theme.** Those justify getting the rubric right independent of today's marginal grade lift. **NET: the themed-HIGH result (mean +0.50 / median −1.70 vs themeless −0.60 / −0.43) stands as a DATA POINT, not a verdict — it is regime-confounded, detector-limited, and scoped to the least valuable use of the axis.** #335's flip decision stays OPEN.

## Operational safety / hardening
- #517 | 2026-08-03 | in_progress | **Data-gated review registry became a PILE — 50 ripe, oldest 72 days, surfacing every Sunday and ignored.** Operator 2026-08-02: *"is this file and review again pile?"* — yes. Measured: **124 registered · 76 pending · 50 ALREADY RIPE**, oldest `trade_stream_stop_placement_without_orders_row` at +72d, 12 of them ≥30d. **Same defect PLAN.md had before the growth ceiling: capture without a forcing function.** ✅ **SHIPPED 8/02 (teeth on the surface, deliberately mild)**: the Sunday *Reviews ready* block now shows **AGE per item** (`[ripe 72d]` — a forgotten review can no longer read as new), a **total count in the header**, a **⚠ banner when any is ≥30d** (*"surfacing is not triage"*), **oldest-first ordering**, and a **render CAP of 8 with the overflow COUNTED not hidden** (an un-scannable wall is WHY it got ignored; silent truncation would be the same failure in a tidier form). ⚠ **Deliberately does NOT auto-close or auto-defer** — a stale review still holds a real question and disposition is the operator's call (the registry's own status semantics); a test asserts the renderer never mutates review state. 8 tests. ✅ **2 CLOSED on evidence**: `time_stop_9m_day2_effectiveness_n10` (superseded — the Day-2 entry was deleted today, nothing left to act on) and `chart_vision_axis_shadow_decision` (the DECISION was signed 8/01 when #343 closed: hold, no promotion). ▶ **REMAINING: 48 ripe need real per-item triage — its own session, not a bulk close.** 8 more look stale by a MECHANICAL test (every referenced task closed: #370 #270 #52 #94 #98 #126 #475 #1) but **that heuristic is NOT proof the question died** — a closed build task can leave a live question, and bulk-closing on it is exactly how things get lost. DoD: each of the 48 read against current state and either answered, closed with a reason, or re-gated with an honest predicate. >> ✅ **TRIAGE SESSION 1, 2026-08-03 — the registry's own HYGIENE first, before reading 48 questions.** ⚠ **Checked the SURFACE before triaging a set nobody sees** (the `/audit`+`/crypto` lesson): `data_gated_reviews.py` renders ONLY `status in (pending, deferred)`. Everything else is skipped **silently** — correct for `done`, a TRAP for anything the schema does not define. **FOUND 7 off-schema entries**: `closed`×3, `shipped`, `resolved`×2, and one — `after_hours_partial_exit_reliability` — with **NO `status` key at all**. ⚠ **HONEST CORRECTION: I first read that last one as a LOST money-path question; it is not.** It carries `closed_on: 2026-05-28` and a full outcome block — genuinely finished, just missing the field. **All 7 were finished, so nothing was lost this time** — but the identical omission on a LIVE question would hide it from every Sunday surface forever with nothing failing. ✅ All 7 normalised to the documented `done` (vocabulary is `pending|done|deferred`). 🔒 **GATED**: `scripts/preflight_yaml_dupe_keys.py` (already pre-commit + deploy `[5e/5]`) now also FAILS on a missing or off-schema status — deliberately narrow, it checks vocabulary membership only and never judges whether a status is the RIGHT one, which is triage and not a gate's job. Proven RED against the pre-fix registry (7 hits), green after. ▶ **THE 48 PENDING RIPE ITEMS ARE STILL UNTRIAGED** — that is the actual remaining work and it is per-item reading, not a sweep. Ordered oldest-first; the head is `ep_selectivity_deep_dive` (now `done`), then `trade_stream_stop_placement_without_orders_row` at +73d. >> 🔬 **EVERY RIPE PREDICATE RUN AGAINST PROD 2026-08-03 — and the pile is HALF what I said.** Ran all 45 date-ripe pending predicates (3 more are predicate-less = date-only). Result: **21 READY (predicate MET) + 3 date-only = 24 genuinely surfaced; 24 WAITING with data simply not accrued; 0 errored.** ⚠ **CORRECTION TO MY OWN 8/02 FRAMING AND TO THIS TASK'S HEADLINE: '48 ripe, ignored' overstated it ~2×** — readiness is `date reached AND count >= threshold` (`data_gated_reviews.py`), and I had counted date alone. ⚠ **THE BIGGER FINDING — the age column is MISLEADING for a whole class.** Several oldest items are TRIPWIRES, not accrual reviews: `orb_entry_stuck_pending_new` (P0, 0/1 **post-fix over 67 days**), `trade_stream_stop_placement_without_orders_row` (0/1 in 14d), `htf_sizing_left_on_legacy_formula` (0/1). Their count is 0 because **the bug has not recurred** — `+73d` is GOOD NEWS rendered as neglect, and closing them would delete a working tripwire. The teeth I shipped 8/02 (age, oldest-first, stale banner) therefore make a never-fired tripwire look like the most neglected item on the board. ▶ **PROPOSED (operator's call, no money): tag each review ACCRUAL vs TRIPWIRE and render age only for ACCRUAL** — for a tripwire show 'no recurrence in Nd'. 🔎 **2 CONCRETE DEFECTS FOUND**: (1) `phase3_telemetry_coverage_check` — its predicate SUMS `catalyst_rubric_scored + theme_context_scored + ep_alert`, i.e. numerator AND denominator together, so it can reach threshold on `ep_alert` alone and **can never detect the failure it exists for**; measured today = 18, **ALL 18 `ep_alert`, ZERO rubric/theme events** — so coverage is 0% and the review would still have read 'not ready'. (2) `breadth_cluster_view_ideation` has **threshold=0**, so it is permanently 'ready' and can never un-ready — noise by construction. ▶ REMAINING: the 24 genuinely-ready items still need per-item reading.
- #513 | 2026-08-03 | deployed | **Monthly backward-check sweep Telegram is unreadable — rebuild it decision-first.** Operator 2026-08-01, reading the 8/01 sweep: *"it contains so much info, not all formatted well, and I have no idea what to do with it. with so much text, it becomes overwhelming, drown with data, so only option is paste it here."* **THE DEFECT**: `quarterly_review` fires 13 scripts and pastes each one's RAW table into Telegram — thousands of chars, no lead, no verdict, no action. **The two items that genuinely needed him were buried mid-message**: (a) the M&A filter suppressed CLRO +358.3% / UMAC +24.1% / WEN +22.8% / LCID +22.6%, flagged MATERIAL-MISS CANDIDATE and requiring HIS FP/TP call (CHANGE_PROCESS r3/r4 — agent must NOT classify); (b) 2 unjustified demotions that then ran, WKC +7.1% and CLF +13.7%, needing his right/wrong label. Everything else was self-concluding (flag detector #92 STRUCTURAL NO-GO; #122 wick N=5<10; #197 cap+1 3/10 accruing; news sources no drift). **BUILD**: (1) a DECISIONS NEEDED block first — only items requiring an operator ruling, each one line with the ask; (2) the money line (traded N · realized P&L); (3) ONE line per check: green / waiting-on-N / needs-you — never a raw table; (4) full tables move to `/audit <topic>` on demand (the surface already exists). ⚠ **Do NOT drop any check or its evidence** — this is a RENDERING change only; every script still runs and still writes its audit row, so nothing stops being measured. ⚠ Telegram: no pipe tables (CLAUDE.md), monospace blocks only, `humanize()` any machine prefix. DoD: a re-run of the 8/01 sweep renders in a message the operator can act on without pasting it anywhere, with both decisions above surfaced in the first block. Pointer: `agents/market_intelligence/quarterly_review.py`, `judge_review.py::format_judge_review`. Refs the ratified report format (memory `report-like-an-exec-summary`). >> ⚠ **PREREQUISITE FOUND 2026-08-01 — `/audit` is NOT in the Telegram menu, so "move the tables to /audit" does not work as written.** Operator asked *"is /audit a real command?"* — verified all three required places: it IS dispatched (`agent.py:5461`) and HAS a handler (`_handle_audit_topic`), but it is **absent from `channels/telegram.py::_register_commands`**, so it works only if you already know to type it. **Swept the whole class rather than the instance: 40 commands are dispatched, 11 are registered — 32 are invisible** (/audit /why /setup /positions /trade /themes /regime /watchlist /missed /htf /flags /parabolic /wick /clusters /cost /ideas /pregame /review /rubric /spotted /sugarbabies /detectors /dryrun /eps /anticipation /promotetheme /unknownrate /watch /datareviews and aliases). This is the documented May-2026 failure class at 5× the size. ⚠ **Do NOT register all 32** — several are pure aliases (/flag+/flags, /review+/reviews, /sugarbaby+/sugarbabies, /datareviews+/reviews) and a 40-item menu is its own overwhelm problem, which is the very complaint this card exists to fix. Register a CURATED set (~8-10 with standalone operator value, aliases deduped), `/audit` mandatory. ⚠ `channels/telegram.py` is ORCHESTRATOR-owned → needs `deploy.sh orchestrator` or `both`, NOT the market-agent default (the #154 scope-drift class that silently dropped /partialnow on 5/28). >> ✅ **REBUILT 2026-08-02 — decision-first.** `_classify` + `_render_digest` replace the 13 raw-table dumps: **NEEDS YOUR CALL first** (explicitly '— none' when empty), then the money line, then ONE line per check (✅ done / ⏳ accruing / 👀 unclassified / 🔴 failed), then `/audit <topic>` for detail. Classification is MARKER-BASED on the scripts' OWN output — re-deriving verdicts here would be a second SoT that drifts. ⚠ **Load-bearing invariant, test-pinned: unclassifiable or empty output → `review`, NEVER clean** — a digest that reports 'all clear' because it failed to parse is the exact failure this rewrite removes. Also pinned: an operator marker BEATS a green verdict in the same output (a script can print both), no raw table survives (<900 chars), failed scripts surface, and EVERY check still appears somewhere. **Nothing stops being measured** — every script still runs and still writes its audit row. ⚖ **Also applied his ruling**: `judge_review`'s `⚠️ UNJUSTIFIED-DEMOTION` heading ASSERTED an error the data cannot show — he reviewed CLF and WKC and ruled the judge CORRECT on both (*"aligned, the stocks looks fine but not true EP moving"*). Renamed to `🔎 DEMOTIONS THAT SUBSEQUENTLY RAN … Not necessarily wrong; the 5d move is not an EP verdict`, with a test asserting 'UNJUSTIFIED' is absent. 13 new tests, suite 4114. ▶ **REMAINING: the /-menu shortlist is NOT shipped — 32 commands are dispatched but invisible and the curated list goes to the operator for approval first (his surface, and a 40-item menu is the same overwhelm this card fixes).**
- #512 | 2026-08-08 | pending | **A job killed by a container restart fails SILENTLY — no audit row, no alert (observed live 2026-07-31).** Restarting apollo-market at 18:42 ET killed `minute_volume_curves_refresh` mid-Polygon-call; it died with `asyncio.exceptions.CancelledError` inside the rate-limit sleep and **wrote nothing** — I checked `mi_audit_log` for `%job%`/`%fail%` in the window and got zero rows. The shutdown races `job_audit`'s failure write, so the row never lands. ⚠ **This is the #381 no-silent-failures class, on the one path where it is least visible**: every deploy is a restart, so any job in flight at deploy time can vanish, and the only trace today was a traceback in the container log that nobody greps. Impact scales with WHAT was clipped — a curves refresh is one stale night, but the same mechanism would swallow a nightly data pull or an audit job just as quietly. DoD: a job cancelled by shutdown leaves a durable record (either `job_audit` catches CancelledError and writes SYNCHRONOUSLY before re-raising, or the boot path reconciles 'started but never finished' runs from the previous process and reports them). Ship the detection first; deciding whether to RE-RUN a clipped job is a separate call. ⚠ Do NOT 'fix' this by draining jobs before shutdown — that lengthens every deploy and the money containers must stay fast to restart. Evidence: this session; `core/job_audit.py:114`; the 18:42 traceback in apollo-market. No money path — observability only.
- #511 | 2026-08-03 | deployed | **Evening brief: the REGIME section restated a standing state instead of reporting a change (operator 2026-07-31: *"regime didn't actually say what changed"*).** Tonight's brief led section 1 with `REGIME — breadth cluster 5/5 red`, said `EP filter ≥75 (unch)`, and closed with `⚠️ Cluster 5/5 red — deterioration` — the same fact twice, no delta, and "deterioration" asserting a direction nothing computed. **ROOT CAUSE: `cluster_fires()` is a STATE predicate, so once a cluster starts firing it re-fires every night with identical text** — it passed the #479 materiality gate on day N the same as day 1, which contradicts that design's own rule that materiality lives at state FLIPS, never standing states. **FIX (display-only, no money): report the TRANSITION.** `get_latest_regime` now fetches CLUSTER_WINDOW **+1** breadth days, so dropping today (`history[1:]`) reconstructs YESTERDAY's exact window — no snapshot table, which is why this didn't need design slice 2. The section is now material only when the cluster STARTS, CLEARS, or its red count MOVES, and it renders the move: `breadth cluster FIRED — 5/5 red (was clear)` · `breadth cluster 4/5 → 5/5 red` · `breadth cluster CLEARED (was 5/5 red)`. An unreconstructable prior is DISCLOSED (`prior window unknown`) and still surfaces the cluster — withholding a live one would be worse. ⚠ A standing cluster stops being material but must not become INVISIBLE: it is carried into the `— no change —` line as `⚠️ cluster 5/5 red (standing)`, so a 5/5-red market cannot quietly drop out on day two. 8 tests; **both halves mutation-checked** — reverting the gate fails `test_STANDING_cluster_is_NOT_material`, reverting the wording fails `test_change_phrase_never_reports_only_the_end_state`. ⚠ My first fixture invented a `{"color": "red"}` shape the real `is_red_day()` never reads — every assertion would have been vacuous while green; it now builds rows through the real predicate (`today_up4`/`today_down4`). Suite 4007. ETA 2026-08-03 = VERIFY-LIVE: Monday's brief renders either a cluster TRANSITION or the standing line, never the old restatement. Refs #479. >> ⚠ **STATUS CORRECTED `deployed`→`in_progress` 2026-07-31 (advisor caught it).** I filed this as `deployed` having only COMMITTED and PUSHED it — no `deploy.sh` ran, so the running market-agent still has the old `_cluster_state` and still fetches only CLUSTER_WINDOW days. **Monday's brief would have rendered the OLD restatement while this line claimed it was fixed** — the precise headline-lie the `deployed` status exists to prevent, and I enforced that same rule on #414 and #184 earlier today. >> ✅ **ADVISOR POINT 2 VERIFIED (the failure mode that would have faked success): the `+1` day DOES reach the composer.** `briefing.py:1359` calls `get_latest_regime(include_breadth_history=True)` and passes the dict WHOLE (`regime=regime`, :1304) — nothing slices it en route, so `_cluster_prior` will not silently return None forever and render "prior window unknown" every night (a path my tests cover, which is exactly why it would have LOOKED fixed). ⚠ I also checked the OTHER consumer I had not considered — the legacy regime formatter at `briefing.py:266` passes the same list straight to `cluster_fires`, which now receives 6 entries instead of 5. **No regression: both `cluster_fires` and `red_count_in_window` slice `monitors[:CLUSTER_WINDOW]` internally**, so the extra day is inert there. The `regime or {...}` fallback at :1378 carries no history — `_cluster_state` reads [] and `_cluster_prior` returns None, so it degrades quietly rather than crashing. ▶ **ETA 2026-08-03 = DEPLOY + verify** (`deploy.sh market-agent`; db.py is execution-loaded so the gate will also ask for `deploy.sh execution` — the change is inert there, operator's call whether to bounce the money container for it). >> ✅ **DEPLOYED 2026-07-31 20:42 ET — `deploy.sh both`, clean.** Market + orchestrator healthy, partition kept 60/removed 29, **zero error events**. Nothing in flight when I restarted (checked jobs RUNNING, not just jobs about-to-start — the miss that clipped `minute_volume_curves_refresh` at 18:42). (b) establish whether ANYTHING re-issues an expired stop at all — `eod_cleanup` at 16:05 cannot (it runs before the expiry), and the next candidate is `live_position_update` at 16:45, UNVERIFIED as a re-issuer; if nothing does, that is the real bug and the gap is ~30 min of unprotected real money EVERY day a DAY-TIF stop expires; (c) confirm the #184b R1 stop-pointer repair relinks an operator-placed manual stop — the reconcile that drives it is cron `hour=9-16 minute=*/15` (scheduler.py:5681) so it had already stopped for the day when the manual order was placed.** ⚖ Trade-state + real money ⇒ CHANGE_PROCESS + sign-off. NOT caused by today's #414 D1 change (that only affects the 9:35 refresh, and QBTS was correctly excluded from it as a same-day entry). Refs #184 (R1 repair), #414, #433. [b3] >> ⚖ **SEVERITY CORRECTED BY THE OPERATOR 2026-07-27: "the 30 MIN is fine given market is closed when this happens." He is right and my framing was wrong.** The 16:15→16:45 gap is NOT the exposure: the market is closed, and a stop order would not execute after hours anyway — so a resting stop bought nothing during that window. **THE REAL RISK IS THE NEXT OPEN.** If nothing re-issues, the position is unprotected at 09:30 the following morning, when a gap CAN hit it — which is exactly the overnight-gap class the daily-loss brake was re-keyed for (#456 F2). So the DoD reorders: **(b) — does ANYTHING re-issue an expired stop before the next open? — is the WHOLE question**, and (a), the false '4:05' promise, is a real but lesser defect (it misleads, it doesn't expose). Tonight is covered ONLY because the operator placed `5201e63d` manually; the systemic question is what happens on a night nobody is watching. ⚠ Also still open at 16:41: the DB row's `stop_order_id` is STILL NULL, so the manual stop is unlinked — the #184b R1 repair runs inside the reconcile whose cron is `hour=9-16 minute=*/15` and had already stopped for the day. If the mirror is still NULL at the next open, stop MANAGEMENT (trail, partials, time-stop) is blind to a position that IS protected — a second, quieter failure mode than the naked one. >> ⚠ **THE NATURAL EXPERIMENT CLOSES TOMORROW (advisor catch) — the open question 'does ANYTHING re-issue an expired stop?' is still UNANSWERED and QBTS will NOT answer it.** The operator's manual replacement `5201e63d` is **GTC**, so it does not expire at 16:15 tomorrow; the free natural experiment is gone and the question then needs either a code read or the next DAY-TIF stop, whenever that occurs. **Answer it by code read tomorrow rather than waiting for another naked position to teach us.** ✅ RESOLVED tonight: the manual stop DID self-heal into the mirror — `stop_order_id` now reads `5201e63d` (was NULL at 16:41), so the #184b R1 repair relinked it and stop management is no longer blind to a protected position. That was the quieter of the two failure modes and it needs no further action. >> ✅ **ANSWERED + FIXED 2026-07-28 by CODE READ (no agent, ~nil budget).** **THE OPEN QUESTION IS ANSWERED: nothing re-issues an expired stop after hours — and it does not matter.** Traced: `_eod_cleanup_job` (16:05) cancels unfilled orders + `sync_positions` ONLY, never re-issues stops; the `stop_ack_timeout_watchdog` is the ONLY re-placer and its cron is **`hour="9-15"`**, so it restores the stop at **09:00 ET — 30 min BEFORE the open**. Bare window = 16:15 → 09:00, **entirely outside market hours, where a stop cannot execute anyway.** ⚖ **SO THE OPERATOR WAS RIGHT TWICE and my filing OVERSTATED it: the after-hours gap is not exposure, AND the next open — the part that would matter — is already covered.** The real defects were narrower, both now FIXED: **(a) the message was structurally false** — it promised a 4:05 re-issue emitted by a handler that only runs AFTER the ~16:15 expiry, so it named a time that had always already passed; now states the truth ("bare overnight; auto-replaced at 9:00 AM ET, before the open. No action needed"). **(b) the L1 alarm demanded 'Immediate operator action: place stop via Alpaca web UI' at 16:28 on a position that was NEVER at risk and would have been auto-covered** — the operator placed one by hand for nothing. Detection was right, the INSTRUCTION was wrong; it now branches on ET market hours and says so. STILL SURFACED either way — silence would be worse. 3 tests pin the real 16:28 case, the intraday case, and the boundaries/weekend. - #464 | 2026-08-04 | deployed | Operational safety / hardening - **Money-path audit R3 (T2 audit 7/12): sync alpaca-py SDK calls block the event loop** — every `client.get_account()/get_order()/place_*` is a bare SYNC call inside `async def` (zero to_thread/run_in_executor in alpaca_client.py, verified); a hung Alpaca endpoint freezes the ENTIRE loop (WS fills + all jobs + the reconcile safety net). Latent-chronic (live since day 1). FIX: wrap money-path SDK calls in `asyncio.to_thread` + explicit `asyncio.wait_for` budgets (or the SDK's async client if suitable); verify no ordering assumptions break (finalizers rely on sequential WS dispatch — to_thread must not reorder handler-internal awaits). Medium refactor, own careful session + G6-style paper exercise. Register: `docs/analysis/moneypath_audit_2026-07-12.md` R3. >> ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-01.** `_sdk()` helper in alpaca_client: `asyncio.wait_for(asyncio.to_thread(...))`, 30s reads / 45s writes. **20 blocking call sites offloaded** (submit_order ×5, get_account ×2, get_order_by_id, get_orders, cancel_order_by_id ×2, replace_order_by_id, get_open_position, get_all_positions, close_position ×2, get_stock_bars ×3, get_stock_latest_trade). ⚠ **My first scope pass MISSED 2 real ones** — I grepped only trading-client methods, so two bare `client.get_stock_bars(request)` data calls survived; the new guard caught them, which is the point of writing the guard before declaring done. **Ordering safety case, stated because it IS the risk:** `to_thread` does NOT reorder awaits WITHIN a coroutine, so read-modify-write sequences keep their order; what changes is that OTHER tasks may now interleave — which is the goal — and the DB side is already guarded by the #151 per-trade advisory locks. A timeout raises to the caller, and every site is already inside try/except returning a failure sentinel, so it degrades to the same path as any other API error. **4 tests**: a scanner that fails on the next bare blocking call anyone adds (mutation-checked; and made comment/docstring-aware after it flagged a docstring that QUOTES the hazard — a guard that cries wolf gets ignored), the helper actually bounds via wait_for, a timeout surfaces as an exception rather than a hang (behavioural), and ordering is preserved within a coroutine (behavioural). Suite 4027. **NOT DEPLOYED** — money-path; ships with the next deploy. ETA 2026-08-04 = verify-live. >> ✅ **DEPLOYED 2026-08-01** — market + orchestrator + execution; `_sdk` present in both money containers, zero errors. ETA 2026-08-04 = verify-live: no `TimeoutError` clusters in the audit log and fills/jobs unaffected across a full trading day.

Verified by query, not by the deploy exiting 0. Zero errors. ETA 2026-08-04 = verify-live: the next day a ticker appears in both modes, the live entry is no longer skipped as `window:duplicate`. >> 🔴 **THE MIGRATION SHIPPED WITH A STALE CONFLICT TARGET — caught 2026-08-03, one day before this line's own verify date.** `5de10cb` (8/01 16:45 ET) DROPPED `mi_live_trades_ticker_alert_date_key` and added the 3-column `..._mode_key`, but `live_tracker._insert_skipped_trade` kept naming the 2-column target. With it missing, the `bar_stream` and `cron_9_31` ORB monitors BOTH ran FTK to completion on 8/03 and the operator got the same skip alert **TWICE** — which is the only reason this surfaced at all. ✅ **FIXED 8/03**: conflict target → `(ticker, alert_date, account_mode)` at all **THREE** sites (the live skip insert + 2 scripts — the third was found by the new guard, not by grep), and the swallow now also writes a `skip_row_insert_error` audit row so a next failure is visible instead of log-only. 🔒 **GUARD `tests/test_live_trades_conflict_target.py`**: every `ON CONFLICT` target on `mi_live_trades` must match a unique constraint `db.py` actually DECLARES — it pairs the migration to the write sites, which is the thing that drifted. WHAT WE HAVE (equities): the ADR-0032 ecosystem board IS this for stocks — ~9700 → themes → 20 ecosystems ranked by breadth-weighted RS (E-CYBR/E-INS...) + ROTATION WATCH (sector turns) + RS leaders; metals/energy appear as miner/ETF equities. DELIVERABLE of THIS task = a DESIGN DOC: the unified strength-map model (a common cross-asset RS frame + an asset-class leadership layer above the ecosystem board + rotation), the surface (brief/hud, NO new command), and a phased slice plan. Net: **no `status='skipped'` row lands in `mi_live_trades`, so `/why` and the EOD filtered-today list silently OMIT every ticker-already-open skip** — the Telegram alert and audit event still fire independently, which is exactly why nobody noticed. Rule simulated = operator's: **sell 1/3 at trigger L, move stop to BREAKEVEN on the rest.** SWEEP vs actual −8.37R (deployed basis): +2% → −1.57R (+6.80) · +3% → −4.70 · +5% → −5.51 · +8% → −4.44 · +10% → −6.82 · 1×ADR → −4.83 (+3.54) · 2R → −5.96. **Every level helps and cost reads 0 — but that 0 is STRUCTURAL, not real: a 0-for-9 cohort has no winners to damage.** The +2%/0.5R rows are top-tick curve-fit (4 trades peaked at exactly +2.1-3.1%). ⚖ **BASIS FINDINGS (the operator's character scope-add):** (1) **fixed-% is wrong by construction, now quantified** — cohort ADR20 spans 2.6-9.7%, so +8% = 0.9×ADR on WULF but 3.1×ADR on WKC; (2) **my hypothesis that R-basis already normalises character is WRONG** — stop distance is 0.15×-1.19× of ADR20 across the 10 trades (the ORB low is one morning's range, not the stock's personality); +2R = +2.2% on MANE vs +12% on NVCR. The simple 'just use R' answer is NOT supported; (3) **ADR-multiple is the right frame** — 1×ADR captures both live runners near their peaks with ONE parameter, and on the paper cohort the ADR variants are the only ones whose benefit doesn't hinge on a single gap-dodge; (4) `character_profile()` checked per instruction and it is the WRONG tool — it profiles MA-respect/pullback depth (trail-side), not run-rate; the fast/slow axis is **ADR%**, whose convention already exists in flag_detector. No new taxonomy needed. 🔴 **THE COST IS REAL AND LARGE (this is the finding that should govern the decision):** replayed on the PAPER winners (+12.23R actual, holds 4-24d), at +3% all 10 trigger and **6 of 10 are BE-scratched mid-hold on days 1-8, winner cohort −2.46R (−20%)** — CRSR scratches day 1 and misses its +12.9% day-2 gap (−1.57R). At 1R: −4.53R. Only at +10% / 2×ADR does the paper cohort stay positive. **The operator's own 'BE stop on day 1 of a 16-day run' worry is confirmed at ~50-60% of triggered winners at low levels.** DEEP TENSION, unresolved at this n: **the levels that best rescue the dead live cohort are exactly the levels that most damage the winners.** STRUCTURAL TRIGGERS: **zero captures on 9 trades** for geometric reasons (prior-day highs all below entry; 20d/52wk highs either already broken at entry or 10-36% away — THC's 52wk sat +2.29% vs a +2.13% peak, missed by 0.16%). Inherent to EP entries. The **gap-up-open** variant fires 6/41 (NVCR d2 +3.5% banks +1.33R near the day's best price) — a plausible COMPLEMENT, never a primary (MANE gapped DOWN on d2). BUILD GAP: price access already exists (websocket seed + 5-min extremes poll), and the stop-replace-then-sell partial path exists but is **gated to 15:45** (`live_tracker.run_partial_exits:691`, #361); `breakeven_active`/ `partial_taken` columns and the `effective_stop=max(...)` composition point exist. Genuinely missing: the intraday trigger EVALUATOR, generalising the partial-sell out of its 15:45 window with idempotency, and ⚖ a new ORDER-EMISSION site = THE LINE, operator ruling required. **Recommended cheapest next evidence step: run this replay as a LOG-ONLY SHADOW on live positions — evaluator only, no order path, no strategy change.** >> 🔑 **FEED FINDING 2026-07-25 — the intraday tracker is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to the class the mechanism must protect, and it decides the shadow's design.** `track_open_position_extremes` (order_manager.py:3735) polls `hour=9-15, minute=*/5` and calls `collector.get_minute_bars(ticker, today, today)` — **Polygon 1-min aggregates**. Granularity (1-min) and cadence (5-min) are both FINE; **the SOURCE is not** — Polygon Starter is the same ~15-17min-delayed feed #489/#490 exist to fix. At the 09:35 poll the available bars stop ~09:18, i.e. BEFORE CRCL's 09:31 fill, so the `b['t'] >= filled_ms` filter returns empty → bare `continue` → nothing written; by the time Polygon publishes 09:31-09:37 (~09:52) the trade closed 09:40:34 and is no longer OPEN, so it is never revisited. **Evidence — the correlation is exact across all 9:** under ~15min CRCL 9m→0.00R · WDFC 9m→0.00 · TSEM 12m→0.00 · HUT 51s→0.00 (WULF 24m→0.48, on the boundary); over ~15min WKC 2.5h→0.90 · THC 3.2h→0.64 · MANE 24h→7.92 · NVCR 30h→2.00. ⚠ **DESIGN CONSEQUENCE: the intraday-partial shadow must NOT be built on Polygon minute bars** — on a delayed feed the evaluator would be blind at exactly the moment it needs to fire. It should read the **Alpaca real-time feed RT-1 shipped today** (the #490 universe overlay already pulls RT prices every scan tick, and `trade_stream` already holds a live websocket for fills; OPEN POSITIONS are a handful of tickers, so RT for them is near-free on infrastructure already deployed). ⚠ ALSO: any existing analytics keyed on `highest_price_seen` UNDERSTATE fast trades — that is what corrupted #503's MFE table. ⚠ **HYPOTHESIS, not yet proven**: inferred from the 9/9 correlation + known Starter behaviour; I could not test `/v2/aggs` latency with the market closed. **MONDAY ACTION (~1 min): during RTH, call `get_minute_bars(<liquid ticker>, today, today)` and compare the newest bar timestamp to now** — that settles it. >> ✅ **SHADOW DESIGN DONE 2026-07-25** (`docs/design/306_intraday_path_recorder_2026-07-25.md`, Fable; reviewed against a second opinion; I verified the claims that correct me). **PATH-recording confirmed over trigger-verdicts** (verdicts lock in a guessed level and need a redeploy to re-test; the path lets every level/basis/segment be swept offline) — with 2 refinements: (i) the recorded table is the LIVE-CAPTURE layer, not an eternal archive — BE-scratch counterfactuals on winners run DAYS past the real exit (paper cohort scratched on day 8), so the review stays hybrid-source: recorded bars for same-day fidelity + Polygon refetch for post-exit extension and anything past the 120d prune. **Net effect: NO schema change, no new table, prune unchanged.** (ii) extremes repair is SUBSUMED, not bolted on. ⚠ **TWO DEFECTS, NOT ONE — my card's root cause was incomplete.** #1 is the Polygon delay; **#2 is the selection predicate**: polling only CURRENTLY-OPEN positions means a trade filled 09:36 and stopped 09:39 ghosts entirely between polls, and EVERY trade loses its final ≤5 min. Fixing only the feed leaves the bug class alive. Predicate is now open-OR-closed-today as a tz-aware TIMESTAMPTZ compare (**no `::date` cast — that is the #310 bug class I confirmed this morning**) + a 16:10 ET sweep for the 15:55-16:00 tail and multi-day healing. ⚠ **CORRECTION TO MY PREMISE:** I told the operator `highest_price_seen` is read by no decision path — **WRONG**, verified: the time-stop scan gates its candidate set on it (`scheduler.py:1247-1279`, excursion <+3%), feeding operator-confirmed `/timestop` OPG sells. Argued safe (time-stop only touches ≥5-day holds where both vendors agree; the sub-15-min trades whose values change are closed and can never enter that population) but the ship note MUST state the provenance change. **On the 2nd opinion's fill convention: DISAGREED, and correctly** — fill-at-bar-CLOSE/VWAP models a fill BELOW the limit price, which is impossible; that's market-on-touch, not a limit. Contract is fill AT trigger L, fill-bar excluded from the scan, pessimistic same-bar tiebreak — which also keeps future sweeps COMPARABLE with the shipped n=9 baseline. **Cadence**: 5-min poll of 1-min bars, each poll refetching the full day-so-far per ticker (≤390 bars, 1 call) with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` — stateless + self-healing across restarts; coverage is a property of the FETCH WINDOW, not poll frequency. Tick/websocket rejected for a shadow (would couple to the entry-critical `bar_stream`); named as the flip-time upgrade. **`track_open_position_extremes` IN scope — SUBSUMED**: same function name, same job id (already in `EXECUTION_OWNED_JOB_IDS`), same cron slot; body becomes fetch-Alpaca → upsert path → compute extremes from the SAME bars. One fetch, one truth. Sharp catch: the Alpaca SDK call is SYNC while today's Polygon fetch is async, so it needs `asyncio.to_thread` or a naive swap would ADD blocking to the execution loop during the ORB window. ⚠ Deploy is **BOTH `market-agent` AND `execution`** (broker/* → two-step, [[deploy-broker-changes-need-execution-step]]). Pre-step: Monday RTH latency probe (STOP only if Alpaca >~3min lag; if Polygon turns out NOT delayed, defect #2 alone still explains the blindness and the design stands). ~9 tests incl. a #310 UTC-rollover regression — **no test currently covers this function at all.** NEXT = Sonnet implementation card off the doc's step-by-step plan. >> ✅ **PATH-RECORDER SHADOW BUILT 2026-07-25 (sonnet off the signed design; I verified every load-bearing claim vs code).** Suite **3685 passed** (3672 baseline + **13 new — the function had ZERO test coverage before**). `track_open_position_extremes` SUBSUMED (same name/job id/cron): body is now fetch-Alpaca → upsert path → compute extremes from the SAME bars; new `get_minute_bars_range` + `persist_intraday_bars` in alpaca_client mirror `_persist_first_bar`'s fire-and-forget `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. New 16:10 ET `position_path_eod_sweep` job, registered in `EXECUTION_OWNED_JOB_IDS`. `asyncio.to_thread` around the sync SDK call (alpaca_client.py:779) so the execution loop never blocks in the ORB window. Selection predicate `(status='filled' AND remaining_shares>0) OR closed_at >= $1` with a tz-aware TIMESTAMPTZ param — **no `::date` cast**, immune to UTC rollover by construction (the #310 bug class); pinned by a regression test that reproduces the old `closed_at::date = today` and shows it wrongly excludes a 20:00 ET close. ⚠ **THE CARD CAUGHT TWO ERRORS IN ITS OWN SPEC — both real, both verified by me:** (1) design Step 3.5 says the extremes upper bound for an OPEN position is 'unbounded', contradicting §4 clamp A and §2b's 16:00 recording boundary — at the 16:10 sweep `now_et`=16:10, so **every still-open position would have had 16:00-16:10 after-hours prints leak into `highest_price_seen`**. Fixed with `window_end_et = min(now_et, 16:00)`, proven by revert-and-fail. (2) **the design's 'only provenance moved' claim is WRONG**: the new window starts 09:30 ET, whereas the Polygon call it replaces returns extended-hours bars (its own docstring: *'Polygon returns extended-hours bars (pre/post-market) when present'* — I confirmed at collector.py:481). So **day-N PRE-MARKET prints can no longer raise `highest_price_seen`** on multi-day holds — a real computation change on exactly the ≥5-day population the time-stop scan gates on. **Direction is fail-safe:** `highest_price_seen` can only go LOWER or stay equal → the `excursion < +3%` filter admits MORE positions → the `/timestop` candidate set GROWS, never shrinks, and /timestop stays operator-confirmed (nothing auto-sells). Arguably the BETTER behaviour (a thin pre-market print is not an excursion you could have exited into) — but it is a behaviour change and the ship note must say so, not claim identical semantics. ⚠ KNOWN GAP flagged by the card, implemented as specified: the sweep's GROUP BY day-coverage query cannot see a day with ZERO recorded bars (absent from the result set entirely). ⚠ Latency probe `scripts/probes/_306_latency_check.py` WRITTEN but **UNRUN** (market closed) — Monday RTH pre-step; STOP if Alpaca lag > ~3 min. **NOT DEPLOYED — needs BOTH `market-agent` AND `execution` (broker/* → two-step, [[deploy-broker-changes-need-execution-step]]), outside 9:25-10:05 / 16:00-17:00 ET.** VERIFY-LIVE = a same-day round trip gets minute rows in `mi_intraday_bars` AND a non-zero `highest_price_seen`. >> ⏱ **BLOCK RE-VALIDATED 2026-07-26 revalidated:2026-07-26 — and the cited gate CANNOT FIRE (stale-block gate).** The block reads 'axes B/C/D sign at the ~8/06 shadow review'. Verified: **`mi_giveback_shadow` = 0 rows** — that review is gated on a round-trip WINNER close and the live cohort is 0-for-9, so 8/06 will surface nothing. Waiting on it is waiting on an event that has not occurred and is not scheduled to. ⚠ **The evidence path has MOVED**: the #306 intraday-partial analysis (7/25) + the path-recorder deployed 7/25 now collect the real intraday data, and the recurring `exit_tune_cohort_review` (n=20/40/60/80/100) is the accrual vehicle — not the 8/06 digest. CONCRETE UNBLOCK CONDITION (restated): the FIRST live round-trip winner close, OR the first exit_tune_cohort_review firing at n=20 — whichever lands first. The sweep found **8 unlabelled call sites, not 1** — every offline eval and replay was ALSO billing the live judge (`tape_judge_eval`, `judge_model_eval`, `judge_enrich_eval`, `judge_backfill_replay`, `selection_replay_268`, `judge_integration_probe`, `chart_axis_shadow`, `chart_axis_eval` are now distinct buckets). **(2) NEW-LANE DETECTOR** (`cost_board._new_lanes_from_rows` + the 17:52 ET watchdog): a caller with spend in the last 3d and ZERO across the trailing 30d Telegrams ONCE, ever (audit-log dedupe, the same idiom the 7/17 budget-alarm re-fire fix used). This closes a blind spot the code NAMED — `compute_caller_cost_anomalies` is cold-start gated *"so a brand-new caller can't false-positive on day 2"*, and the reduction heuristics only ask whether EXISTING spend is reducible, never whether a lane should exist. 28 tests. ⚠ **DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT: a lane/purpose registry mapping each caller to what it buys** — it needs a "who consumes this" field nothing can verify, and #517 (filed TODAY) is live evidence that a registry without a forcing function becomes a pile. The weakest honest claim (*this started spending*) beats a richer one that rots. ✅ **VERIFIED-LIVE 2026-08-02 (deploy `0903575`, market-agent + execution).** Ran `compute_cost_watchdog` against REAL `api_usage` inside `apollo-market` (read-only — only the 17:52 ET job writes): clean run, 0 anomalies / 3 opportunities / **0 new lanes**, and CROSS-CHECKED against the raw table independently of the detector — the 5 most-recently-first-seen callers are all 6-17 days old, so zero is the CORRECT answer, not a silent failure. `judge_divergence` (first seen 7/27) is the worked example: it would have been announced on day one had this existed. ▶ **DEFERRED TO A WEEKDAY (flagged by the 8/02 /simplify altitude pass, NOT applied — money path, Sunday night, live judge grades at 09:45 ET):** the fix landed one layer too HIGH. `grade_holistic` now requires `log_caller`, but the true chokepoint is `judge_transport.invoke_forced_tool` — the function that actually calls `spend_tracker.log_anthropic_call_safe` — which still has `log_caller: Optional[str] = None`. **The residual hazard is WORSE than the one fixed**: a future forced-tool judge built directly on the transport (not via `grade_holistic`) would log NOTHING — no bucket at all, rather than the wrong bucket — and the AST test can't see it, because it hardcodes the three judge entry-point names. Verified: BOTH production call sites (`ep_grade_judge`, `mgmt_judge`) already pass a literal, so requiring it is zero-behaviour-change. ⚠ It is NOT free: `None = no logging` is a documented signed contract (#377, 'byte-identical to the pre-#377 path') with a test pinning it (`test_judge_logs_nothing_when_log_caller_none`), so this is a CONTRACT change, not a cleanup — it needs the deliberate deploy window, not a late-Sunday push. ⏭ ALSO CONSIDERED AND SKIPPED: moving the dedupe query into `db.py` per the CLAUDE.md single-SoT-for-queries rule. Two reviewers independently said leave it — `cost_board.py` already holds 3 inline cost-specific queries, and moving only the newest would split cost queries across two files, which is worse than the inconsistency it fixes. ▶ REMAINDER of #313 unchanged: replay-everything CI (#302) + spend-vs-P&L attribution.

The Stage-2 BLOCKING fork is NOT taken — deliberately, on evidence.** The shadow had recorded **ZERO** `exposure_family_breach` events since it shipped 7/12, and that zero is HONEST, not broken instrumentation — I checked the acting population per [[shadow-zero-effect-check-instrumentation]]. **The live book has never been full:** 2026-05-01→07-27, on days holding anything — max concurrent **4** (once), **average 1.50**, only **4 days** with 2+, **1 day** with 3+. At threshold 2 an emit needs two EXISTING same-family positions plus the candidate = three of a book that has exceeded two positions twice ever. **So the premortem R1 risk ('5 same-family EP HIGHs fill all 5 slots = one bet at ~100% deployment') is real in principle and UNOBSERVED in practice — the binding constraint today is book SIZE, not correlation.** Shipping a blocking cap now would guard a zero-instance risk while adding a new failure mode to the entry path; that fork was explicitly declined.
